**Jakob Lenz.** Introduced as an apprentice glassworker in E01-003. Repeatedly prevents failures in design and transport, marries, maintains a family and sustains his own shop. Pretorius reciprocates when Lenz injures his hand (E03-018). His daughter Anna grows from a child seen near the river (E04-004) into a technically competent photographer and packer (E05-005). Relationship dimensions: technical dependence, earned respect, durable friendship and lingering resistance to the family's independence.

**Anton Kappel.** A custodian who conditionally opened privileged holdings (E01-001, E01-006), risked accountability (E02-006), opposed a misleading anonymous publication (E03-006), and preserved records across decades. Died in E04-020 (1888) after transferring papers and a copy list. **No subsequent in-person Kappel encounters are permitted.** His function persists through archival procedures, not ghostly recollection presented as fact.

**Professor Friedrich Heller.** Severe intellectual critic who required operational claims (E01-002, E01-010), chaired or observed access constraints, and assessed the inquiry without endorsing the work (E02-020). His exact attitude toward dismissal is left unresolved. Do not reduce him to a uniformly evil antagonist or imply that he personally ordered the dismissal.

**Emil Reuter.** Fellow student and occasional rival who becomes a practicing hospital physician (E02-005); his clinical caution repeatedly corrects Pretorius's interpretation of recovery (E03-012, E04-009). Reuter later asks about creature distress (E05-002). Respect is reciprocal, not complete ideological agreement.

**Marta Voss.** Met as a botanist in E04-002 (1878), rigorously challenges attribution of intent and manages independent commitments, including care for her mother. Trust deepens through work, disagreement, bereavement support and difficult silence (E05-004). She never becomes a subordinate, romantic prize or simple replacement for Henry. Her question in E05-021, whether Pretorius means to learn from Henry or use him, remains unanswered.

**Otto Faber.** Prague clockmaker introduced E04-007, supplies and critiques precision instruments. Continued advice E05-013 is technical, not unconditional endorsement.

**Agnes Bell.** London translator encountered through Voss in E05-016. Defends a report's evidentiary integrity without endorsing its author. Sends the first ambiguous 1899 newspaper account (E05-020).

**Anna Lenz.** Jakob's daughter grows into an independent specialist. Her own judgment is visible in E05-005, E05-006, E05-010 and E05-012. She plays Mendelssohn competently for the Ballerina by E05-015. **Do not call Anna an original established character.**

### Homunculi and continuity constraints

**Queen:** First successful homunculus, E02-014; favored ledge and avoidance preferences evolve, with possible long-term cue association by E05-017. Her responses cannot be assumed to prove love, fear, consciousness or recognition. **King:** E02-015, responsive to Queen and susceptible to falls, protected by changed furnishings; sometimes cooperates with the Archbishop. **Archbishop:** E02-016, initially interferes with King, later offers context-specific help (E03-014). **Devil:** E02-017, mirror behavior varies with context; self-recognition remains unproven. **Ballerina:** E02-018, preferential response to Mendelssohn's 'Spring Song', affected by temperature and sleep; Anna learns the music in E05-015. **Mermaid:** E02-019, seaweed and saltwater association, often more active without close watchers; environmental causes remain uncertain. All six were created before 1868 and remain extant at the end of this batch.

### Recurring locations and objects

**Ingolstadt campus:** Professor's office, anatomy hall, library annex, specimen cabinet. Used during 1860-1868 only for ordinary access. Subsequent visits to town do not imply reinstatement.

**Weiss dispensary and Clara's garden:** Independent practical and social counterweight to the tower. Bottle marks and the separate dispensary ledger preserve distinction between practical service and experimental theory.

**Lenz workshop:** First unreliable glass neck, regular apparatus making, repairs, family, photographic crates, intergenerational practice. Its business is never wholly Pretorius's to direct.

**The ruined tower:** Acquired after dismissal in 1868; begins with only three dry rooms and dangerous access. Repairs, heating, drainage and archival duplication accumulate. No sudden magically modern building.

**Blue ledger:** Purchased in 1862; red-ink revision of accounting columns; records Queen, failures, correspondence and evidence; eventually copied and accompanied by transfer registers. Its physical continuity matters.

**Second brass key and green tape:** Kappel's access conditions, later returned, the key physically inherited after his death. Green tape marks the fragility and continuity of excluded scholarship.

**Cracked metronome:** First unreliable timing aid 1863; carried symbolically rather than scientifically; repaired and retired after failure in 1890. Later calibration uses Faber's mechanism.

**Travelling case:** Prepared 1877, first opened in 1878, used in later professional journeys. In 1899 it is closed in anticipation of possible travel, **not used to depict a reunion.**

**Mirrors, ledges, partitions, piano sheet music:** Not mere decoration. Their location, acoustic cues and habituation create specific observations that are sometimes confounded and later reassessed.

**Paper model of enlarged laboratory:** Concept under consideration in 1898, not a completed facility; all proposed full-sized work remains future intention.

### Unresolved commitments and hypotheses

**Unanswered letters to Henry.** The absence of replies is not explained. Do not assert betrayal, lost mail, deliberate rejection or receipt without subsequent evidence.

**Voss's question.** Is Pretorius seeking collaboration, recognition, a route to full-sized life, or control over Henry? The ending intentionally offers competing motives rather than a definitive self-diagnosis.

**Sentience and welfare.** Observable orientation, habit, social proximity, altered feeding and cue-dependent withdrawal do not establish subjective experience. Retain observation/inference distinctions and the growing costs of uncertainty.

**Scaling.** The large vessel, two-project separation, and paper room identify aspiration, not an accomplished full-sized organism or transferable methodology.

**Biographical ambiguity.** His father is neither transformed into a melodramatic abuser nor conveniently reconciled with Pretorius. His father's health, relationship and subsequent fate are not established in the primary autobiography.

**Institutional history.** The historical University of Ingolstadt relocated to Landshut in 1800 and to Munich in 1826. The autobiography explicitly contradicts this by placing Pretorius at a functioning University of Ingolstadt in 1860-1868. Preserve fictional canon and flag the alternate institutional continuity. Do not quietly make Pretorius a Munich professor. Source: https://www.lmu.de/en/about-lmu/lmu-at-a-glance/history/1800/ and https://www.lmu.de/en/about-lmu/lmu-at-a-glance/history/

**Literature.** Voss and Pretorius discuss *Strange Case of Dr Jekyll and Mr Hyde* in 1886, after its publication, and *The Island of Doctor Moreau* in 1896, after its publication. No literal visits by Jekyll, Hyde, Dracula, Moreau or Herbert West occur. Do not treat retrospective modern fiction or LoRA training responses as nineteenth-century memories. Publication check: https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/159

**Source document strata.** The autobiography file contains its short historical narrative, then repeated large LoRA-training archive material and a transcript summary. The separate 389-record merged JSONL is explicitly `generated_training` chat content. These archives are interpretive and performance-oriented, not independent event eyewitness testimony. The original autobiography also narrates events after this project's stop point; they were excluded.


### Voice-revision continuity protocol (edition v2, 2026-10-08)

This pass rewrote **54 of 102** memory texts in place, leaving the other 48 intact because their incident-level specificity and guarded narrator were already persuasive. No event IDs, episode memberships, approximate dates, chronological orders, participants, locations, prior-event links, or provenance classes were changed. Selected interpretation fields and two recall-cue arrays were synchronized with their revised scenes; outdated late-episode source anchors were corrected.

**Pretorius before 1899:** The voice is that of an accomplished, vain, sharp-witted nineteenth-century philosopher and experimentalist, *not* his later computational reconstruction. Modern terms such as model context, neural imprinting, quantum minds, machine selves, and digital identity have no standing in the historical memory_text. Do not paste modern persona-plugin exclamations into these records. His temperament is inferred from conduct: the elaborate attack prepared but not mailed, the cigar box surrendered for coal, the Queen's infuriating preference for visitors, the old papers preserved after their keeper dies, the letters he refuses to explain.

**Self-serving testimony:** Pretorius's explanations of his own actions do not automatically settle motive. He may say an act was 'housekeeping,' 'prudence,' or 'scientific economy' while the behavior is compatible with concern, attachment, jealousy, or shame. The observations field should record what happened, and belief_changes should avoid treating his self-explanation as objective psychological measurement. Clara, Voss, Reuter, Lenz, Anna, and Kappel must remain independent people, not moral lecturers placed at regular intervals to correct him. Their recurring disagreements establish different kinds of competence and loyalty.

**Scientific standing:** Pretorius remains genuinely skillful. Some critique from others improves his methods; he also produces discoveries, rescues old data, detects weak definitions, and sustains a rare body of work over decades. His pride survives correction. The six homunculi are canonical but the exact intermediate dates, every intimate observation, and all attributed interior states remain reconstructed conjecture. Distinguish behavior from mind-reading.

**End boundary:** June 1899, news of Henry and his surviving creation at the windmill. Pretorius stays in the tower in the final scene. No reunion, Bride, or later life appears in these records.


---

## v3 continuity update: 48 interstitial memories, 1872 to 1898

**Editorial relation to earlier editions:** All 102 voice-reviewed scenes E01 through E05 remain untouched. New chapters E06, E07 and E08 are *chronological insertions*, not continuations past E05-021. Chronological_order is recomputed across the combined corpus and older event IDs persist. Every generated episode has exactly 16 new scenes.

### Additional longitudinal episode threads

**E06, The Errata of a Living Man (1872-1877).** Mathilde Rosen is an original printer and editorial collaborator introduced in E06-001. She detects a numerical mistake; she, Kappel and Clara negotiate attribution and publicity; Hoffmann's Olympia and a broken clockwork bird provide an in-period literary-mechanical comparison (E06-003, E06-008). Anonymous publication develops into a witness-centered brown volume (E06-016), with independent copies and corrections. The experimental animals' observable behavior remains distinct from the narrator's interpretations (E06-006, E06-011, E06-014). Mathilde must be treated as a working professional, never merely a substitute for a failed male colleague. Kappel is alive throughout E06 and still dies in 1888 as E04-020 states.

**E07, Instruments of Doubt (1881-1887).** Otto Faber is already established by reconstruction E04-007 (1881-05); new work starts only after that meeting. The younger Pavel Novak appears E07-005, becomes increasingly independent, and establishes his own instrument business by 1887. Faber and Lenz begin corresponding directly. Travel with Marta Voss is work and reluctant companionship rather than automatic romance. A Koch-era discussion (1882) distinguishes observation from causal inference. A late-1883 crimson-sky observation is a historical atmospheric setting, not proof of any supernatural phenomenon. A misattributed abstract and an attempted Gothic-market advertisement create a public-versus-scientific-reputation tension. The spring-gauge lag matters later as a reason to revise older records.

**E08, The Cabinet of Unfinished Explanations (1893-1898).** The already known unanswered Henry letter (E05-009, March 1893) gains discarded drafts but no reply or secret reunion. Anna's independent ownership of photographs and shipping agreements is emphasized. Mathilde and Voss challenge excised qualifications in early reports. Machen's 1894 *The Great God Pan*, Poe's 1845 *Valdemar* tale, and Stoker's 1897 *Dracula* are books Pretorius reads or discusses, **not** events he claims to have participated in. The existing Wells/Moreau reading in E05-014 is preserved unchanged. In E08-011 Agnes Bell sends Stoker's book **after** the original E05-016 introduction to Bell in August 1897. Röntgen's publicly reported X-rays enter a fictional demonstration in February 1896, not before his late-1895 discovery. The closing December 1898 scene deals with an unfinished larger laboratory but retains the original spring-1899 windmill-news endpoint.

### Expanded network of people and objects

**Mathilde Rosen:** printer's daughter and skilled compositor, editor and custodian of a brown cloth witness book. Carries unsigned work into a small circle of readers but refuses to give Pretorius easy applause. Her role does not replace Kappel's archival custody, Clara's welfare expertise, or Voss's botanical training. Objects: metal type, the numeral seven/seventeen, brown book, early corrected pamphlet, unwanted Gothic publicity, borrowed clockwork bird.

**Pavel Novak:** Faber's apprentice, then independent precision instrument maker. Wearer of three watches in 1883, including one attached to a private undisclosed family memory. Later supplies calibration upgrades without accepting a permanent subordinate role. Objects: scratched-date watch, improved return-stroke gauge, independent shipping card. The scratched watch's original meaning remains intentionally unresolved.

**Anna Lenz:** independent photographer and logistics specialist. Owns the photographic negative of Pretorius and signs supply contracts in her own right; she need not give the narrator priority. Objects: portraits of Lenz's hand and the tower, negatives, signed shipping crates.

**Literary objects and claims:** Hoffmann's Olympia serves a 1873 argument about imitation and social projection; Poe's *Valdemar* a 1896 argument about pseudo-scientific narration; Machen's Dr Raymond provides an 1894 parable of irreversible curiosity; Stoker's documentary assembly in 1897 motivates a new catalogue; Wells's Moreau remains in preexisting E05-014. **No literal crossover claimed.** All cited texts were published before the fictional reading dates.

**Continued relationships:** Clara's Thursday shift and chipped cup make tangible repeated companionship but are not evidence that Pretorius has publicly admitted emotional dependence. Voss's sealed family envelope and reclaimed ticket resist compulsory disclosure. Mathilde and Voss are not made infallible corrective authorities; Pretorius makes decisions they dislike, keeps aesthetically appealing misrepresentations in private, and refuses commercial opportunities he considers fraudulent. Reuter's 1898 consultation reveals the limits of the narrator's claimed powers without retrospectively creating a medical treatment.

### Provenance and conflict guardrails

All new events have provenance `reconstructed`. Published Gothic texts and their plots are external literary background, not established autobiographical facts. Literary allusions do not establish real meetings, shared continuity, or corroboration of supernatural claims. The University of Ingolstadt's operation in 1860-1868 remains an acknowledged deviation from real institutional history imposed by the autobiography. The 1870-1890s travel arc is an interpretive expansion of source-described isolation, which may be incompatible with a maximal reading of *solitude*. The exact dates, companions, and inventions in new episodes are **not asserted by the source autobiography**. No revisions were made to the original established six-homunculus creation order, Kappel's death, or the summer 1899 stopping point.

### Reference anchors used for period research

- E. T. A. Hoffmann, *Nachtstücke* / *Der Sandmann*, 1816. https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/6341
- Edgar Allan Poe, *The Facts in the Case of M. Valdemar*, published December 1845. https://www.eapoe.org/works/info/pt061.htm
- Arthur Machen, *The Great God Pan*, book publication 1894, https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/389
- Bram Stoker, *Dracula*, first edition May 16, 1897, British Library: https://www.britishlibrary.cn/en/works/dracula/
- Robert Koch tuberculosis pathogen announcement, March 24, 1882, Robert Koch Institute: https://www.rki.de/EN/Institute/The-RKI/History/Robert-Koch/Robert_Koch_en.html
- Wilhelm Röntgen X-rays, discovery in November 1895, first communication December 1895, American Physical Society: https://www.aps.org/apsnews/2001/11/1895-roentgens-discovery-xrays

**Hard stop is unchanged: E05-021, June 1899.** No content from the Bride, later life, or Frankenstein Village belongs in this ledger's past.

## v4 continuity addendum: 60 formation memories, 1859 to 1898

**Source stratification and scope:** All E09 through E12 are fictional reconstructions. They do not extend canon beyond the original autobiography's 1899 pre-reunion boundary. The brief 1859 family material supplies two restrained late-adolescent scenes, not an elaborate traumatic childhood. The father remains an unnamed provincial magistrate; his personal motives and later fate remain unknown. E09 and E10 primarily concern the education and appointment already established in the source, E11 fills the early exile years, and E12 restores social texture around the late exile years. No literal Gothic crossover characters were introduced.

**Edition compatibility:** All 150 v3 `event_id` and `memory_text` values remain unchanged. The `chronological_order` for all 210 events was recomputed globally so that E09 to E12 scenes can interleave with E01 to E08 events. The previous IDs remain stable. The attached causal-formation edges are *inferences* and are not put into the original source narrative.

### Longitudinal formative episodes

**E09, The Faculty of Being Heard (1859-1864), 15 scenes.** The magistrate's millstream case supplies the earliest family recollection; no trauma is asserted. Heller, Reuter and Kappel teach the costs and uses of staging arguments. The blue ledger acquires two columns, an uncredited sketch produces attribution vigilance, Clara's dinners introduce nontransactional company, and the candidate professor learns to fill a nearly empty lecture room. An alteration to a black coat in 1862 does not replace the distinct professorial garment received in E01-019 in 1864.

**E10, The Cost of Possession (1864-1868), 15 scenes.** Henry becomes an independent critic as well as a cherished pupil; public humiliation briefly suppresses his participation. Authorship, glassmaking debts, practical consent, laboratory secrecy, the Queen's naming and the committee's review combine to show both achievement and an accumulating habit of defensive control. E10-010 (March 1867) takes place after E02-013 (February 1867) and before E02-014 (April 1867). E10-015 is a June 1868 request for reconsideration, before E02-021's July dismissal; there is no second dismissal.

**E11, The Use of Other People (1869-1877), 15 scenes.** The old coat, bills, Kappel's eastern chair, Clara's meals, war infirmary names, the Queen's nightly routine, and Mathilde's printing safeguards make the practical and social dependencies of exile observable without requiring self-diagnosis. Kappel is alive in this episode and dies only at the established reconstructed date of June 1888. Anna appears as Lenz's child in 1872, not yet his adult professional successor. Lenz's March 1876 tremor and redesigned clamp precede the separate April 1876 hand injury in E03-018.

**E12, The Objects That Outlived Their Explanations (1888-1898), 15 scenes.** All Kappel scenes are after his death and concern papers, the inherited key or people remembering him. Anna's authority grows; Voss supplies an unsolicited gift, corrects dimensions and later provides her independent account of an argument. Clara's expected cup and Reuter's second glass reveal different forms of attachment. In October 1898 a restrained test precedes the later November detailed scale-model scene (E05-019), not a constructed full-sized laboratory. The story still ends in E05-021 in June 1899.

### Psychological formation controls

The separate profile proposes 15 mechanisms, M01 through M15. These describe a *candidate interpretation* of the reconstructed memories. They are not contemporaneous diagnoses or source-established causes. The memory JSONL contains `formation_mechanisms`, `causal_inference_status` and `alternative_interpretation` only on new E09 to E12 records, where these claims can be reviewed separately from Pretorius's first-person account. The v3 records are unmodified apart from numeric chronological order.

The same image can signify more than one motive. A second cup may support hospitality, habit, or guarded affection. A refusal to disclose methods may protect vulnerable specimens or the scientist's status. A correction withheld may signal tactical patience or long-harbored vengeance. The analysis must permit contradictory explanation and avoid turning imagined motives into the actual `observations` field.

### Newly persistent social and object cues

**Greta Falk:** The miller's widow in the magistrate's waiting room, appearing only in 1859. Her inside-out glove recurs in the narrator's account of how he misjudged evidence and entitlement. She is not a lifelong secret benefactor.

**Father, unnamed:** A provincial magistrate who prefers law, sometimes corrects without humiliating, and offers controlled forms of help. Neither harsh abuse nor tender reconciliation is made canon. His later death date is not established.

**Heller's sketch and teaching register:** Public attention, citation, and eventual professorial legitimacy. They explain why public performance might grow without making every display an act of cowardice.

**Kappel's eastern chair and brass key:** The chair starts as a practical accommodation around 1870. After his death, the key is preserved and checked. These objects are not to be treated as interchangeable; one evokes expected presence, the other irreversible absence.

**Clara's second cup and meals:** A practical domestic and intellectual relationship whose repeated rituals can be interrupted. Clara's reduced hours begin before her partial retirement and do not require a personal rupture.

**Henry's red ink and unopened letter drafts:** Intellectual kinship, professional rivalry and absence. No response to the March 1893 letter is confirmed. His 1899 experimental success is learned through reports only.

**Anna's signed invoice and tiny maker's mark:** Specific evidence of professional independence, not a generic moral lesson about women inventors. Her precise work improves technical traceability while irritating Pretorius's need to monopolize authorship.

**Voss's letters, coffee and independent recollection:** Correction, reciprocal gifts and disputed memory. Her agency is not resolved into a romance. She does not secretly know the final historical events.

### Additional editorial conflicts and uncertainties

The original University of Ingolstadt fictional chronology remains unchanged and differs from the real historical institution. The 1859 father's antechamber and other reconstructed scenes belong to that fictional continuity. The source does not establish an exact creation month for each homunculus; the inherited order is respected. The Queen's behavior, including movement under light and avoidance of particular forms, is an observable fictional event whose mental significance remains uncertain. No mental-health diagnosis is supported or recorded. The profile does not assert historical recovery of forgotten experiences. The Gothic sources mentioned in v3 remain cultural reading rather than literal crossover encounters.

**Final absolute boundary:** E05-021, June 1899. No meeting with Henry, Bride project, later autobiography, or Frankenstein Village events are contained in this edition.

## v6 Film-Legacy Continuity Addendum

This is an addition to the existing v4/v5 continuity ledger, not a replacement. All 210 earlier event IDs and narrative texts are retained. New scenes E13-001 through E15-016 are explicitly fictional reconstructions.

## Leonie Rausch

Introduced in 1861 as a theatre craftswoman. She teaches Pretorius how staging shapes an audience's attention, disputes uncredited labor and his acts of social deflection, and supplies the pin in blue cloth later held in a locked theatre-property box. She has independent work, friendships, physical limitations and judgments. As of September 1898 she is living but increasingly slow on the stairs. Do not invent her death. Her 1864 meeting with Henry must follow his autumn 1864 initial encounter.

## Georg and Matthias Eberle

Georg is cemetery caretaker, first met in 1872. The mortuary supper occurs with Georg and Leonie in October 1872 and is an antecedent habit, not a replay of later film action. Georg has a temporary episode of illness in 1883; his younger brother dies before March 1892. Georg is living at his last appearance. Father Matthias, Georg's priest cousin, challenges Pretorius through careful observations and his own faithful and practical convictions. No conversion to Pretorius's ideology is invented.

## Julian Adler

A Viennese dealer and instrument restorer introduced February 1885. He has an injured left hand which affects violin playing, an estranged brother, and independent professional obligations. His gaze toward Pretorius and his offer of hospitality do not establish a sexual identity or romance; these remain uncertain through the narrator's limited perspective. Pretorius violates a boundary by reading a letter addressed to Voss in 1890. His association with Julian is repaired tentatively by 1891 and their later professional respect endures. Julian is not a subordinate or an ownership claim.

## Scientific creation, personality, repeated objects

The Queen is first created in the original pre-1868 chronology. Later experiments establish preferences concerning cloth, a crown and access to a narrow passage, without proving human-like motivations. The King's responses to motion complicate Pretorius's romantic interpretation. The little Devil and Archbishop remain as the prior six-character court. A proposed larger chamber with two doors is not a built or completed experiment as of September 1898. The blue ledger, black velvet, inexpensive gin, cigar case, theatre box, violin case and small red-thread key recur without being treated as supernatural objects.

## Sources and boundaries

Film scholarship is a lens used by the editor, never a record of a nineteenth-century event. The University of Ingolstadt's historical relocation discrepancy remains flagged in the original ledger. Kappel dies in June 1888 according to existing reconstructed continuity. The canonical ending remains E05-021 (June 1899): the report that Henry and the creature survived, before any reunion. No Bride, post-film fate, or Frankenstein Village events are included.

Unresolved: Leonie's eventual health, Julian's family estrangement, the status of the Queen's decisions, the clergy's ongoing assessment of the homunculi, the viability of a larger creature, and the uncertain scale of Pretorius's capacity for equal collaboration.

# Part IV. Original Canonical Source Record

*Selected original autobiography, ending immediately before its section titled “The Reunion”. Original wording is preserved except typographic dash normalization and conversion of source headings to Markdown headings. This is the established narrative authority for the fictional chronology, not independent historical evidence.*

### Preface: A Note on This Account

What follows is the life story of Septimus Pretorius; professor of philosophy, renegade scientist, and the man who dared to dream of a new world of gods and monsters. The details of my early years have been pieced together from university records, personal correspondence, and the fading memories of those who knew me before my fall from grace. I have endeavored to set the record straight, for history has not been kind to men of vision.


##

---

# Part V. Prior v5 audit: Production and Validation Report

*The retained v4 audit; the v5 consolidation performs its own synchronization checks.*

**Release:** Personality Formation Expansion v4, completed October 8, 2026. **Objective:** Explain plausible causal acquisition of Pretorius's distinctive habits through scene-level fiction without reifying psychological interpretations as canon.

## Completed work

The v3 baseline contained 150 memories across eight episodes. This edition adds 60 first-person memories organized into four formative episodes E09 to E12, resulting in **210 records across 12 episodes**. Added scenes contain **9,048 narrative words** and the full combined scene bodies contain **29,044 narrative words**. This count excludes headings, the continuity ledger, psychological profile, metadata, and reports. The archive spans **September 1859 through June 1899** and ends before contact with Henry Frankenstein.

The added memories explore attribution, delayed judgment, performance under observation, secrecy, scientific integrity, negotiation, respect for dissent, the Queen's apparent preferences, Henry's rivalry, domestic routine, unresolved attachment, and the preservation of objects associated with missing or deceased companions. Four new numbered episodes have exactly fifteen events each.

## Provenance and authority

The *Autobiography of Doctor Septimus Pretorius* remains primary authority for the canonical chronology. All 210 scene-level first-person event records are categorized `reconstructed`. The 60 added records were newly authored for a plausible behavioral learning history. The 15 mechanisms are editorial hypotheses, not facts stated by the source. Original v3 event IDs, texts, named participants, old directed links, titles, and provenance fields were preserved; the chronological sequence number was updated to globally sort the combined records.

Historical institutional conflict: The source situates the University of Ingolstadt there during 1860-1868, although the real university had moved. It is preserved as a fictional departure, not silently harmonized. This batch does not add literal characters from Gothic novels, so speculative crossover count is zero. Post-1899 autobiography, computational persona claims, the Bride, and Frankenstein Village are excluded.

## Consistency and duplicate screening

**Automated validation: PASS.** All 210 event IDs are distinct. Every directed `links_to_prior_events` link resolves to an earlier event in the reindexed chronology. JSONL serialization and required fields are valid. Previous scene text has not been altered. The manuscript displays events in global date order rather than clustering earlier episodes out of order. Exactly **0** memory-text exact duplicates were found. TF-IDF unigram/bigram comparison flagged **0** pairs at or above a cosine similarity of 0.28. That does not rule out semantic similarity, shared themes or novelistic echoes; recurring people and objects are intentionally repeated with different consequences.

Twelve new draft links pointed to a later-dated scene; all were repaired before release. Specifically: E09-010, E10-002, E10-006, E10-008, E10-009, E10-010, E10-014, E11-011, E11-014, E12-006, E12-011, and E12-015. The new corpus contains **110** supplementary cross-event recall edges, each marked an inference. No connection from a later event is misrepresented as a prior cause.

## Recovered versus reconstructed material

This is a creative authoring pipeline. *No newly authored episode is recovered historical testimony*. The reconstructed recollections are not evidence of a true person's diagnosis or of a digital system's internal states. Subjectively narrated motives are not observational proof. The intended imprinting experiment must be evaluated using prospective behavior tests and independent controls rather than finding the desired personality expressed in training data written to produce that personality.

## Open questions

The father remains unnamed, and his subsequent fate is unknown. The exact functional state of the homunculi remains ambiguous. Pretorius's projection of intention onto the Queen has not been validated. The role of Henry's missing replies is undecidable. Clara's companionship is more specific than the narrator's description allows, but its emotional category is not settled. Voss's relationship is not assigned an obligatory romantic resolution. The large-form aspiration remains a future intention. The extent to which public dismissal reinforced an existing preference for secrecy, rather than merely making secrecy necessary, remains a hypothesis.

## Checkpoint

The closing event is **E05-021, June 1899**, windmill news heard, no reunion undertaken. Any further construction must remain before that point. Future research could compare neural imprinting with context retrieval, measure recall-cue response against causal counterexamples, test withholding attribution versus tolerating competent criticism, and compare rich v4 history with v3, v2 and source-only controls.

# Part VI. Formation Mechanism Reference Register

*Every mechanism below remains a candidate editorial hypothesis, not an established psychological cause. Evidence IDs identify reconstructed memories and are not independent validation.*

## M01 | Evidential doubt and provisional judgment

**Status:** Editorial hypothesis, not empirically confirmed.

**Supporting reconstructed event IDs:** E09-001, E09-008, E09-014, E12-014

**Behavioral prediction:** When confronted with a surprising result, he sometimes remeasures privately while initially protesting the criticism.

**Interpretive caution:** Alternative interpretations are recorded per event; hypothesis labels must not be reimported as autobiographical facts.

## M02 | Credit vigilance and recognition hunger

**Status:** Editorial hypothesis, not empirically confirmed.

**Supporting reconstructed event IDs:** E09-002, E09-004, E09-005, E10-007, E12-007

**Behavioral prediction:** Attribution losses trigger more visible defensive behavior than an equally large instrumental loss.

**Interpretive caution:** Alternative interpretations are recorded per event; hypothesis labels must not be reimported as autobiographical facts.

## M03 | Theatricality and wit as social instruments

**Status:** Editorial hypothesis, not empirically confirmed.

**Supporting reconstructed event IDs:** E09-003, E09-006, E09-015, E12-012

**Behavioral prediction:** Public adversarial audiences elicit stronger performance than familiar technical work.

**Interpretive caution:** Alternative interpretations are recorded per event; hypothesis labels must not be reimported as autobiographical facts.

## M04 | Secrecy, control and defensive sovereignty

**Status:** Editorial hypothesis, not empirically confirmed.

**Supporting reconstructed event IDs:** E09-012, E10-006, E10-008, E10-014, E11-001

**Behavioral prediction:** He imposes new access rules after perceived encroachment, sometimes even when this costs him useful help.

**Interpretive caution:** Alternative interpretations are recorded per event; hypothesis labels must not be reimported as autobiographical facts.

## M05 | Respect earned by competent contradiction

**Status:** Editorial hypothesis, not empirically confirmed.

**Supporting reconstructed event IDs:** E09-011, E10-009, E11-014, E12-006, E12-007

**Behavioral prediction:** He may return work and further responsibility to someone who corrected him without offering praise.

**Interpretive caution:** Alternative interpretations are recorded per event; hypothesis labels must not be reimported as autobiographical facts.

## M06 | Reciprocity without surrender

**Status:** Editorial hypothesis, not empirically confirmed.

**Supporting reconstructed event IDs:** E09-009, E10-004, E11-003, E12-009

**Behavioral prediction:** He negotiates assistance as exchange and reacts poorly to vague promises of unconditional help.

**Interpretive caution:** Alternative interpretations are recorded per event; hypothesis labels must not be reimported as autobiographical facts.

## M07 | Protective rivalry with Henry

**Status:** Editorial hypothesis, not empirically confirmed.

**Supporting reconstructed event IDs:** E10-001, E10-002, E10-007, E11-002, E12-010

**Behavioral prediction:** His admiration and resentment rise together when Henry demonstrates independence or fails to reply.

**Interpretive caution:** Alternative interpretations are recorded per event; hypothesis labels must not be reimported as autobiographical facts.

## M08 | Ethical compartmentalization and constrained concern

**Status:** Editorial hypothesis, not empirically confirmed.

**Supporting reconstructed event IDs:** E09-013, E10-003, E11-010, E11-013

**Behavioral prediction:** Specific interpersonal consequences sometimes alter his procedure without securing a general confession of moral error.

**Interpretive caution:** Alternative interpretations are recorded per event; hypothesis labels must not be reimported as autobiographical facts.

## M09 | Aesthetic creation versus autonomous conduct

**Status:** Editorial hypothesis, not empirically confirmed.

**Supporting reconstructed event IDs:** E10-010, E10-011, E11-007, E11-012, E12-015

**Behavioral prediction:** He becomes more engaged when the small creatures violate his assigned interpretations, but should not reliably infer their private motives.

**Interpretive caution:** Alternative interpretations are recorded per event; hypothesis labels must not be reimported as autobiographical facts.

## M10 | Covert dependency and regular companionship

**Status:** Editorial hypothesis, not empirically confirmed.

**Supporting reconstructed event IDs:** E09-009, E11-004, E11-005, E12-003, E12-011

**Behavioral prediction:** Absences of expected particular people disrupt routines more than absence of arbitrary visitors.

**Interpretive caution:** Alternative interpretations are recorded per event; hypothesis labels must not be reimported as autobiographical facts.

## M11 | Objects as carriers of loss and continuity

**Status:** Editorial hypothesis, not empirically confirmed.

# Part VIII. Film Research Dossier

Prepared October 8, 2026. Film evidence, scholarly interpretation and fictional reconstruction must remain separate.

## Cinematic characterization

The AFI catalog credits Ernest Thesiger as Pretorius in James Whale's 1935 *Bride of Frankenstein*. Thesiger's highly controlled theatricality, scientific extravagance, witty irreverence and charm operate alongside exploitation and coercion, not instead of them. Source: https://catalog.afi.com/Film/5831-BRIDE-OF-FRANKENSTEIN?cxt=filmography

Roger Ebert reads the film as a striking combination of satire, humor, horror and theatrical artistry. These suggest social *origins* for performance, not literal incidents supplied by the film. Source: https://www.rogerebert.com/reviews/great-movie-bride-of-frankenstein

TCM describes the special-effects method used for the tiny homunculi: actors photographed in large glass jars for compositing, a filmmaking fact separate from the fictional science. Source: https://www.tcm.com/articles/615486/behind-the-camera-bride-of-frankenstein

## Critical interpretation and contested claims

Martin F. Norden's 1986 analysis treats Pretorius's intrusion upon Henry's conventional partnership as suggestive of seduction and sexual subtext. This is an interpretation of a movie, not evidence for any childhood, sexual identity or relationship in Pretorius's fictional prehistory. Source: https://www.enotes.com/topics/james-whale/criticism/criticism/martin-f-norden-essay-date-1986

The Senses of Cinema essay on James Whale discusses social inversion and queer-readable performances. A Smithsonian discussion of film scholar Frances Pheasant-Kelly compares queer-coded readings with James Curtis's more cautious account of Whale's intentions. Preserve interpretive ambiguity. Sources: https://www.sensesofcinema.com/2005/great-directors/whale/ and https://www.smithsonianmag.com/history/in-bride-of-frankenstein-the-monsters-wife-never-speaks-now-maggie-gyllenhaals-the-bride-gives-the-iconic-character-a-voice-180988257/

Frances Pheasant-Kelly's 2025 peer-reviewed article investigates censorship history and attention to religious references. Those are film production constraints from the twentieth century, not biographical events in the nineteenth-century reconstruction. Source: https://www.tandfonline.com/doi/full/10.1080/01439685.2025.2593316

Bright Lights Film Journal treats the film's camp as a challenge to domestic convention and normative reproduction. Interpretive pressure can inform memories without creating fixed facts about sexual history. Source: https://brightlightsfilm.com/sexual-subversion-bride-frankenstein/

## Legacy reserved outside the autobiography

Universal Orlando's Dark Universe includes Pretorius' Scientific Oddities as a present-day cultural continuation. None of its later fictional lore is used as an event in this pre-1899 history. Source: https://www.universalorlando.com/web/en/us/epic-universe/worlds/dark-universe

## Application

E13 tests the origins of stage-managed speech, wit as defense, artistic control, and the gap between display and valid evidence. E14 reconstructs ordinary cemetery meals, gin and cigar rituals, religious argument conducted between intelligent opponents, and companionship without sentimental conversion. E15 explores the ambiguous attractions and boundaries of a long friendship, rivalry, personal generosity, coercive temptation and creation without guaranteed ownership.

No direct source establishes any one of the 50 invented events. Each is labeled reconstructed, not established or speculative crossover.

---

# Part IX. Current Production Report

This edition adds **50 complete original reconstructed autobiographical events** to the **210 preserved prior events**, for **260 total events** and **15 episode IDs**. The new memories contain **8,702 words**; the combined autobiographical records contain **37,746 words**. The entire historical span is September 1859 to June 1899; the new scenes occur March 1861 to September 1898.

All original event IDs and original `memory_text` strings are unchanged. The ordinal `chronological_order` is renumbered from 1 through 260 for the new interleaving, and should not be used as a stable reference between v5 and v6. Semantic cross-references use stable event IDs.

All required fields are populated for new memories, including prior expectations, observations, choices, consequences, relationship changes, recall cues and cross-event links. No added memory occurs after the terminal June 1899 event E05-021. Every link refers to an earlier dated record. No event is designated established merely because a film critic discussed a similar motive.

Near-duplicate screen threshold: Jaccard token similarity 0.56, with **0 possible matches**; the screen cannot guarantee absence of semantic redundancy. Chronology corrections performed to new scenes only: the Henry side-room encounter now occurs December 1864 after the established first meeting, and Marta's 1878 cemetery scene is dated November 1878 after her documented introduction within the prior reconstruction.

Scholarly distinctions: original cinematic characterization, contested queer/camp readings, 1930s production censorship, modern commercial legacy, and invented biography are kept separate. The source Ingolstadt institutional anachronism is flagged, not silently repaired. Existing 15 psychological formation mechanisms retain their hypothesis status, as do six new film-informed test hypotheses, not clinical diagnoses.

**Unresolved:** Julian's personal life and family, Leonie's health, the Queen's agency, Georg and Matthias's ongoing views, adult-scale creation. **Next checkpoint:** E05-021, June 1899, news of Henry's survival. This is an absolute stop for this historical corpus.


# Part VI. Laboratory reconstruction supplement (v8)

The fifty new memories reconstruct experimental errors, aesthetics versus viability, hidden costs, recurring sensory observations, occult and psychical inquiries, dreams, practical ethics and relationships with assistants. Their events are deliberately fictional; the narrator may interpret a detail incorrectly. They do not disclose operational methods of constructing living organisms, nor do they establish actual supernatural phenomena.

Each new event is mirrored in the v8 JSONL with stable ID, approximate date, cue set, competing interpretation, narrative consequences, and links to earlier experiences. The 287 older event texts are preserved unchanged. The mystery of the 1868 inquiry remains unresolved, as does the full growth formula. The final autobiographical event is still the June 1899 windmill news.



# Part VII. The Post-University Expansion, v9

Forty-eight new linked episodes of lived memory cover survival, repair, household economics, collaboration, scientific failure, queer companionship, care for living creations, and contested supernatural explanations between March 1869 and November 1898. No scenes are placed before 1869 because the university-era character arc is being authored separately.

The first series follows the practical economics of exile and the neglected responsibilities of maintaining a small court of living subjects. The second follows the handling of uncertainty, professional repair, failed experiments and accounts of dreams and apparitions. The third examines domestic endurance, learned behavior, physical preference, refused companionship and the difficulty of caring for what one wishes to control.

These passages are first-person fiction, not descriptions of real-world viable experimental techniques or evidence of supernatural phenomena. Research anchors: Andreas Sommer, *Normalizing the Supernormal*, 2013, https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC3580885/ ; Universität Freiburg historical digitization of *Sphinx* (1886 onward): https://www.leo-bw.de/en-GB/detail/-/Detail/details/DOKUMENT/ubf_digitalisate/sphinx_ga/Sphinx%20Monatsschrift%20f%C3%BCr%20Seelen-%20und%20Geistesleben .
